AMM vs. other optimizers (same functions and budgets)

# Adaptive Multi Mode: Budget-Aware Routing of Optimizers By Online Landscape Probes

Benjamin Jacob Bodner

![](images/a85702df0b38dafb39fcf7b2b1a3bd1738893b95ff31d1241c9d58f441dec2f9.jpg)  
Figure 1: Held-out bbob instances 101–110 (f1–f24), equal budgets; field runs, SLSQP included, restart until the budget is spent or the final target is reached. Points: share of 51 targets reached per run, averaged over 10 instances and 120 function–dimension cells (� = 2–40). HCMA (published COCO archive, own instances): � = 2–20 only; there AMM is level at 10·� (+.016) and leads by +.10 to +.17 from 30·�. TPE ran every � only at 10·�. NADE (2025 MA-BBOB winner) and ERADS (van Stein’s 2024 algorithm): authors’ code, run after the final test. Against the pre-registered field AMM is highest in 382 of 600 function–dimension–budget cells (236 outright, 146 tied; 380, 234 outright, with NADE and ERADS added) and leads IPOP-CMA-ES by +.121 to +.312.

## 2 Related work

Table 1 contrasts AMM with the closest routin<sub>g</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>roaches. Sin-<sub>g</sub>le classical solvers and restarted or surro<sub>g</sub>ate-assisted CMA-ES variants each own a bud<sub>g</sub>et band (A<sub>pp</sub>endix D); AMM uses such <sub>so</sub>l<sub>vers unc</sub>h<sub>ange</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ms on</sub>l<sub>y a</sub> b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er a</sub>ll<sub>oca</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> k<sub>nown</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Fixed hand-overs and races. HCMA opens with NEWUOA for 10·�, then races STEP a<sub>g</sub>ainst surro<sub>g</sub>ate-assisted BIPOP-CMA-ES (Loshchilov et al., 2013). Hansen (2019) com<sub>p</sub>ared l<sub>q</sub>-CMA-ES with a portfolio that runs SLSQP (sequential least-squares quadratic pro-<sub>g</sub>rammin<sub>g</sub>) before CMA-ES; AMM’s <sub>p</sub>refix (the route for bud<sub>g</sub>ets u<sub>p</sub> to 100·�, Section 3.1), which <sub>p</sub>icks restarted SLSQP or, on a ru<sub>gg</sub>ed readin<sub>g</sub>, an evolution strate<sub>gy</sub> (at 100·�, surro<sub>g</sub>ate-ranked <sub>p</sub>o<sub>p</sub>ulations), descends from it. COCO<sub>p</sub>f, MOS and EGO-CMA switch or chain en<sub>g</sub>ines b<sub>y</sub> realised im<sub>p</sub>rovement or a fixed rule (Baudiš, 2014; LaTorre et al., 2011; Mohammadi et al., 2015). So do memetic <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>m<sub>es suc</sub>h <sub>as</sub> MTS <sub>a</sub>nd SHADE-ILS<sub>, w</sub>h<sub>ose c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ce</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>wee</sub>n <sub>a</sub> coordinate search and L-BFGS-B is closest to AMM’s (Molina et al., 2018; Tsen<sub>g</sub> and Chen, 2008). All decide b<sub>y</sub> schedule or b<sub>y</sub> which en<sub>g</sub>ine im<sub>p</sub>roved.

A-priori wizards. Shiwa and NGOpt select by hand-written rules on <sub>p</sub>roblem metadata (Liu et al., 2020; Meunier et al., 2022). ## 3.1 Routing by dimension and budget

Algorithm 1 first applies three whole-budget gates (lines 2–11), <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>can</sub> <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>o</sub>l<sub>e</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>eng</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>any</sub> <sub>gen-</sub> eral search. From � = 5 and 100·�, the <sub>p</sub>air test at the start <sub>p</sub>oint (line 5) sends a se<sub>p</sub>arable readin<sub>g</sub> to STEP (select the easiest point) restarts, the separable route, and, from 300·�, a cou<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub> of onl<sub>y</sub> adjacent coordinates that the band check confirms to a decoupled coordinate search, the chain route. At � = 2–3 from 100·�, a basin readin<sub>g</sub> on a Sobol desi<sub>g</sub>n (line 9) sends several smooth basins to MLSL (multi-level sin<sub>g</sub>le linka<sub>g</sub>e) and, u<sub>p</sub> to 300·�, a sin<sub>g</sub>le smooth basin to one SLSQP descent first (line 11). A <sub>g</sub>ate that does not fire costs onl<sub>y</sub> its evaluations, and its b<sub>es</sub>t <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ays</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>run</sub> <sub>con</sub>ti<sub>nues</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ar</sub>t <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t <sub>or, a</sub>ft<sub>er a</sub> b<sub>as</sub>i<sub>n rea</sub>di<sub>ng,</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gn</sub>’<sub>s</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>escen</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f li<sub>ne</sub> 11 <sub>ran.</sub>

Up to 100·� the prefix spends the whole budget (line 12; Algorithm 2) on a desi<sub>g</sub>n, STEP and the curvature <sub>p</sub>robe, which <sub>p</sub>icks restarted SLSQP or a (1+1)-CMA-ES; from � = 5 above 30·� a <sub>p</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>eau</sub> r<sub>ea</sub>din<sub>g se</sub>nd<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> r<sub>es</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> IPOP-<sub>a</sub>CMA-ES <sub>popu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>s, a</sub>nd u<sub>p</sub> to 30·� two rules can hand it to restarted COBYQA (Table 6, rules (i)–(iii)). Above 100·�, the ATM sam<sub>p</sub>ler, a <sub>p</sub>o<sub>p</sub>ulation-based random search (Section 3.3), takes a fifth of the bud<sub>g</sub>et (at most 60·� from � = 10; line 13), and STEP, with a se<sub>p</sub>arabilit<sub>y</sub> check, half of the rest (line 14). If the check fails, from $D = 5$ th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>es</sub> (line 16) send <sub>p</sub>lateau and ru<sub>gg</sub>ed readin<sub>g</sub>s to IPOP-aCMA-ES <sub>p</sub>o<sub>p</sub>- ulations, smooth readings to an SLSQP stage and, from $D = 2 0$ fractal readings to restarted SLSQP. ## 3.3 Engines

The en<sub>g</sub>ines are SciP<sub>y</sub>’s SLSQP (Kraft, 1988), L-BFGS-B (B<sub>y</sub>rd et al., 1995) and COBYQA (Ra<sub>g</sub>onneau, 2022; Ra<sub>g</sub>onneau and Zhan<sub>g</sub>, 2026; Virtanen et al., 2020); a scaled finite-diference BFGS after the nonsmooth BFGS of Lewis and Overton (2013); STEP, a round-robin coordinate search in the st<sub>y</sub>le of BSrr (Pošík and Baudiš, 2015; Swarzber<sub>g</sub> et al., 1994); MLSL from Sobol <sub>p</sub>oints (Rinnoo<sub>y</sub> Kan and Timmer, 1987); and Nelder–Mead restarts at $D \ \leq \ 2$ (Nelder and Mead, 1965). The <sub>p</sub>ool also holds a (1+1)-Cholesk<sub>y</sub>-CMA-ES (I<sub>g</sub>el et al., 2006) with active u<sub>p</sub>date (Arnold and Hansen, 2010) <sub>a</sub>nd <sub>qua</sub>dr<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>-m<sub>o</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>eps,</sub> IPOP-<sub>a</sub>CMA-ES <sub>a</sub>nd th<sub>e</sub> ATM <sub>sa</sub>m<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub>r (Bodner, 2019), a <sub>p</sub>o<sub>p</sub>ulation-based random search with success-<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ep con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>.</sub> O<sub>n</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n rou</sub>t<sub>e uses a</sub> d<sub>ecoup</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub> search<sub>,</sub> the one en<sub>g</sub>ine not run alone in Section 5.2. A<sub>pp</sub>endix B.2 lists ever<sub>y</sub> settin<sub>g</sub>.

## 4 Development process and experimental design

## 4.1 The development loop

The develo<sub>p</sub>ment lo<sub>g</sub> of AMM lists 71 c<sub>y</sub>cles. From com<sub>p</sub>osite c32 on, a c<sub>y</sub>cle finds a losin<sub>g</sub> cell (a function, dimension and bud<sub>g</sub>et where AMM trails the field ofSection 4.3) on develo<sub>p</sub>ment instances and traces its mechanism. It then <sub>p</sub>re-re<sub>g</sub>isters a fix (a <sub>p</sub>robe and an en<sub>g</sub>ine<sub>,</sub> the slice and numeric ran<sub>g</sub>e of its <sub>g</sub>ain<sub>,</sub> the acce<sub>p</sub>ted losses and the decision rule), screens it on develo<sub>p</sub>ment instances and <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>rms</sub> it <sub>once</sub> <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t it <sub>on</sub> <sub>never-use</sub>d bb<sub>o</sub>b instances, which are then spent. A fix is kept, or promoted, only if its <sub>p</sub>rediction held; <sub>p</sub>romoted fixes enter the next com<sub>p</sub>osite (c23–c46 in the code; c47 was the last candidate). AI codin<sub>g</sub>-a<sub>g</sub>ent sessions (Claude Code) <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osed the fixes, wrote the <sub>p</sub>re-re<sub>g</sub>istrations, and <sub>ran</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ana</sub>l<sub>yse</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>au</sub>th<sub>or</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d <sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>approve</sub>d ever<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>re-re<sub>g</sub><sup>i</sup>strat<sup>i</sup>on an<sup>d</sup> <sub>p</sub>romot<sup>i</sup>on.

## 5 Results

## 5.1 Final test on the oficial instances

Ever<sub>y</sub> arm ran once on instances 101–110<sub>,</sub> and ever<sub>y</sub> run returned a finite value within its bud<sub>g</sub>et (6,000 runs <sub>p</sub>er arm; 2,880 for TPE’s slice). AMM has the hi<sub>g</sub>hest tar<sub>g</sub>et share in 382 of the 600 cells (b<sub>y</sub> bud<sub>g</sub>et in Table 3 and Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 1, <sub>p</sub>er dimension in Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 3), 236 outri<sub>g</sub>ht and 146 tied (115 ties at a share of 1, all tar<sub>g</sub>ets reached, and 25 at 0). No other method leads in more than 23 cells at an<sub>y</sub> bud<sub>g</sub>et. It i<sub>s cre</sub>dibl<sub>y a</sub>h<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f <sub>every compe</sub>tit<sub>or</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>ran</sub> th<sub>e ce</sub>ll i<sub>n</sub> 110 <sub>ce</sub>ll<sub>s,</sub> <sub>c</sub>r<sub>e</sub>dibl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>e</sub>hind in 35 <sub>a</sub>nd n<sub>e</sub>ith<sub>e</sub>r in 455<sub>; w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t HCMA’<sub>s a</sub>r<sub>c</sub>hi<sub>ve</sub> levels it is hi<sub>g</sub>hest in 415 (288 outri<sub>g</sub>ht). Rotated, anytime and cheap problems. Coordinate-wise routes <sub>canno</sub>t fi<sub>re on ro</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>separa</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d f<sub>a</sub>ll b<sub>ac</sub>k <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e pr</sub>i<sub>ce</sub> of their <sub>p</sub>robes. On rotated f3 and f4 (Section 5.3) the best of IPOP-, BIPOP-CMA-ES, SLSQP and DE leads AMM by more than .02 in 4 of the 25 cells of each (in none unrotated). ## B.2 Engines

Restarted en<sub>g</sub>ines restart from uniform <sub>p</sub>oints until their share is spent. COBYQA starts with a trust radius of 0.25 of the box width (0.1 after rule (iii) of Table 6). The scaled finite-diference BFGS (“scaled BFGS” in the tables; one en<sub>g</sub>ine for the smooth and the kink routes) follows Lewis and Overton (2013). A pass of the loop. On f13, bbob’s sharp-ridge function, SLSQP stalls at a kink. A first fix (a smaller SLSQP step, predicted to gain +.04 to +.15 in tar<sub>g</sub>et share) <sub>g</sub>ained +.028 with an interval that included zero and failed a<sub>g</sub>ain on a retest (<sub>p</sub>redicted +.02 to +.08). The redesi<sub>g</sub>n (a scaled finite-diference BFGS, <sub>p</sub>redicted +.10 to +.35) <sub>g</sub>ained +.304 and was ke<sub>p</sub>t (Table 7).

The 2019 entry ofthe ATM sampler, reproduced. The first quasi-Newton comparison changed the design. When restarted SciPy SLSQP was added to the development field (ids 61– 70), c23 had been <sub>p</sub>redicted to lead it b<sub>y</sub> +.10 to +.25 in the <sub>p</sub>rimar<sub>y</sub> score. Instead SLSQP led c23 b<sub>y</sub> .032 [.018, .047], on the smooth f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f6 <sub>an</sub>d f8<sub>–</sub>f14 <sub>an</sub>d <sub>on</sub> G<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>ag</sub>h<sub>er</sub>’<sub>s</sub> f21 <sub>an</sub>d f22<sub>, a</sub>t b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s</sub> u<sub>p</sub> to 300·�. Ever<sub>y q</sub>uasi-Newton route of AMM was added after this result, and SLSQP is in the field and in the SLSQP-then-IPOP-CMA-ES run<sub>g</sub> of the ladder (Section 4.3).

## D Related work, continued

One classical solver per budget band. NEWUOA beats BIPOP-CMA<sub>-</sub>ES <sub>on sp</sub>h<sub>ere- an</sub>d R<sub>osen</sub>b<sub>roc</sub>k<sub>-</sub>lik<sub>e</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d<sub>, res</sub>t<sub>ar</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> G<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>ag</sub>h<sub>er</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>ons,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>no</sub>t <sub>e</sub>l<sub>sew</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> <sub>a</sub>t ti<sub>g</sub>ht t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>s</sub> (Hansen and Ros, 2010). Under restarts, Nelder–Mead wins in low dimension and <sub>q</sub>uadratic models in hi<sub>g</sub>her (Pošík and Hu<sub>y</sub>er, 2012);

Powell’s BOBYQA, NEWUOA and LINCOA trail SciPy’s SLSQP and BFGS (Brockhof and Villain, 2025).

Restart and surrogate CMA-ES. IPOP- and BIPOP-CMA-ES restart CMA-ES with <sub>g</sub>rowin<sub>g</sub> or interleaved <sub>p</sub>o<sub>p</sub>ulations (Au<sub>g</sub>er and Hansen, 2005; Hansen, 2009), and NBIPOP-aCMA-ES ada<sub>p</sub>ts the s<sub>p</sub>lit (Loshchilov et al., 2012a). l<sub>q</sub>-CMA-ES, saACM-ES and DTS-CMA-ES add surrogates (Bajer et al., 2019; Hansen, 2019; Loshchilov et al., 2012b). Each is one mechanism inside one evolution strate<sub>gy</sub>; AMM runs (1+1)- and IPOP-CMA-ES (Hansen, 2016) as en<sub>g</sub><sup>i</sup>nes.

Automated and LLM design. LLaMEA generates optimizers with a lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e model and evaluates them on bbob or MA-BBOB (van Stein and Bäck, 2025), and BLADE benchmarks such <sub>g</sub>enerators (van Stein et al., 2025). Per-function tunin<sub>g</sub> of a modular CMA-ES, validated on <sub>u</sub>n<sub>u</sub>sed instances<sub>,</sub> matches or im<sub>p</sub>roves the BBOB-2009 <sub>p</sub>er-function virtual best on half of the functions (de Nobel et al., 2026).

Replay and hand-over. Each stage replays the smaller plan exactly <sub>up</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>arger</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anges.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>as</sub>t <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>aye</sub>d <sub>p</sub>lan has 6,400 evaluations from � = 5 and 5,120 at � = 2. At the h<sub>an</sub>d<sub>-over,</sub> th<sub>e po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>so</sub> f<sub>ar rep</sub>l<sub>ace</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>res</sub>h <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>or</sub>di<sub>nary</sub> <sub>run</sub>’<sub>s</sub> <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ases</sub> <sub>wou</sub>ld d<sub>raw:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pre</sub>fi<sub>x</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gn,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sam-</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>er</sub>’<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>popu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> CMA<sub>-</sub>ES’<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>covar</sub>i<sub>ance an</sub>d th<sub>e res</sub>t<sub>ar</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d SLSQP’s samples. Why state is recomputed.
